| page\_type                                                  |

| AudioCategory                                               |
| path  target                                                |
| Shows how to create apps that use various audio categories. |

Shows how to create apps that use various audio categories.

> **Note:** This sample is part of a large collection of UWP feature samples. You can download this sample as a standalone ZIP file [from docs.microsoft.com](https://docs.microsoft.com/samples/microsoft/windows-universal-samples/audiocategory/), or you can download the entire collection as a single [ZIP file](https://github.com/Microsoft/Windows-universal-samples/archive/master.zip), but be sure to unzip everything to access shared dependencies. For more info on working with the ZIP file, the samples collection, and GitHub, see [Get the UWP samples from GitHub](https://aka.ms/ovu2uq). For more samples, see the [Samples portal](https://aka.ms/winsamples) on the Windows Dev Center.

1.  If you download the samples ZIP, be sure to unzip the entire archive, not just the folder with the sample you want to build.
2.  Start Microsoft Visual Studio and select **File** > **Open** > **Project/Solution**.
3.  Starting in the folder where you unzipped the samples, go to the Samples subfolder, then the subfolder for this specific sample, then the subfolder for your preferred language (C++, C#, or JavaScript). Double-click the Visual Studio Solution (.sln) file.
4.  Press Ctrl+Shift+B, or select **Build** > **Build Solution**.

The next steps depend on whether you just want to deploy the sample or you want to both deploy and run it.

**Deploying the sample**

1.  Select **Build** > **Deploy Solution**.

**Deploying and running the sample**

1.  To debug the sample and then run it, press F5 or select **Debug** > **Start Debugging**. To run the sample without debugging, press Ctrl+F5 or select**Debug** > **Start Without Debugging**.

using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
using PlaybackManager;

namespace SDKTemplate
{
    public partial class MainPage : Page
    {
        // Change the string below to reflect the name of your sample.
        // This is used on the main page as the title of the sample.
        public const string FEATURE_NAME = "Audio Category Sample";

        // Change the array below to reflect the name of your scenarios.
        // This will be used to populate the list of scenarios on the main page with
        // which the user will choose the specific scenario that they are interested in.
        // These should be in the form: "Navigating to a web page".
        // The code in MainPage will take care of turning this into: "1) Navigating to a web page"
        List<Scenario> scenarios = new List<Scenario>
        {
            new Scenario() { Title = "Movie", ClassType = typeof(Scenario1) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Media", ClassType = typeof(Scenario2) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Game Chat", ClassType = typeof(Scenario3) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Speech", ClassType = typeof(Scenario4) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Communications", ClassType = typeof(Scenario5) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Alerts", ClassType = typeof(Scenario6) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Sound Effects", ClassType = typeof(Scenario7) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Game Effects", ClassType = typeof(Scenario8) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Game Media", ClassType = typeof(Scenario9) },
            new Scenario() { Title = "Other", ClassType = typeof(Scenario10) }
        };
    }

    public class Scenario
    {
        public string Title { get; set; }
        public Type ClassType { get; set; }
    }
}

Contents of https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/Windows-universal-samples/main/Samples/AudioCategory/cs/AudioCategory/PlaybackControl.xaml.cs:
//*********************************************************
//
// Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.
// THIS CODE IS PROVIDED *AS IS* WITHOUT WARRANTY OF
// ANY KIND, EITHER EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING ANY
// IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR
// PURPOSE, MERCHANTABILITY, OR NON-INFRINGEMENT.
//
//*********************************************************

using Windows.UI.Xaml;
using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
using Windows.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
using SDKTemplate;
using System;
using Windows.UI.Xaml.Media;
using Windows.UI.Core;
using Windows.Foundation;
using Windows.Media;

namespace PlaybackManager
{
    /// <summary>
    /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
    /// </summary>
    public sealed partial class PlaybackControl
    {
        // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
        // as NotifyUser()
        MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;

        CoreWindow cw = Window.Current.CoreWindow;
        bool IsPlaying = false;

        SystemMediaTransportControls systemMediaControls = null;

        public PlaybackControl()
        {
            this.InitializeComponent();

            // add new handlers
            systemMediaControls = SystemMediaTransportControls.GetForCurrentView();
            systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged += SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;
            systemMediaControls.ButtonPressed += SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed;
            systemMediaControls.IsPlayEnabled = true;
            systemMediaControls.IsPauseEnabled = true;
            systemMediaControls.IsStopEnabled = true;
            systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Closed;
        }

        ~PlaybackControl()
        {
            if (systemMediaControls != null)
            {
                systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged -= SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;
                systemMediaControls.ButtonPressed -= SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed;
            }
        }

        async public void Play()
        {
            await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                OutputMedia.Play();
                systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Playing;
            });
        }

        async public void Pause()
        {
            await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                OutputMedia.Pause();
                systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Paused;
            });
        }

        async public void Stop()
        {
            await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                OutputMedia.Stop();
                systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Stopped;
            });
        }

        public void SetAudioCategory(AudioCategory category)
        {
            OutputMedia.AudioCategory = category;
        }

        public void SetAudioDeviceType(AudioDeviceType devicetype)
        {
            OutputMedia.AudioDeviceType = devicetype;
        }

        public async void SelectFile()
        {
            Windows.Storage.Pickers.FileOpenPicker picker = new Windows.Storage.Pickers.FileOpenPicker();
            picker.SuggestedStartLocation = Windows.Storage.Pickers.PickerLocationId.MusicLibrary;
            picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".mp3");
            picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".mp4");
            picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".m4a");
            picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".wma");
            picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".wav");
            Windows.Storage.StorageFile file = await picker.PickSingleFileAsync();
            if (file != null)
            {
                var stream = await file.OpenAsync(Windows.Storage.FileAccessMode.Read);
                OutputMedia.AutoPlay = false;
                await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
                {
                    OutputMedia.SetSource(stream, file.ContentType);
                });
            }
        }

        async void DisplayStatus(string text)
        {
            await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                Status.Text += text + "\n";
            });
        }

        string GetTimeStampedMessage(string eventText)
        {
            return eventText + "   " + DateTime.Now.Hour.ToString() + ":" + DateTime.Now.Minute.ToString() + ":" + DateTime.Now.Second.ToString();
        }

        //If your app is playing media you feel that a user should not miss if a VOIP call comes in, you may
        //want to consider pausing playback when your app receives a SoundLevel(Low) notification.
        //A SoundLevel(Low) means your app volume has been attenuated by the system (likely for a VOIP call).

        string SoundLevelToString(SoundLevel level)
        {
            string LevelString;

            switch (level)
            {
                case SoundLevel.Muted:
                    LevelString = "Muted";
                    break;
                case SoundLevel.Low:
                    LevelString = "Low";
                    break;
                case SoundLevel.Full:
                    LevelString = "Full";
                    break;
                default:
                    LevelString = "Unknown";
                    break;
            }
            return LevelString;
        }

        async void AppMuted()
        {
            await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                if (OutputMedia.CurrentState != MediaElementState.Paused)
                {
                    IsPlaying = true;
                    Pause();
                }
                else
                {
                    IsPlaying = false;
                }
            });
            DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Muted"));
        }

        async void AppUnmuted()
        {
            await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
            {
                if (IsPlaying)
                {
                    Play();
                }
            });
            DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Unmuted"));
        }

        void SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged(SystemMediaTransportControls sender, SystemMediaTransportControlsPropertyChangedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (e.Property == SystemMediaTransportControlsProperty.SoundLevel)
            {
                var soundLevelState = sender.SoundLevel;

                DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("App sound level is [" + SoundLevelToString(soundLevelState) + "]"));
                if (soundLevelState == SoundLevel.Muted)
                {
                    AppMuted();
                }
                else
                {
                    AppUnmuted();
                }
            }
        }

        void SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed(SystemMediaTransportControls sender, SystemMediaTransportControlsButtonPressedEventArgs e)
        {
            switch (e.Button)
            {
                case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Play:
                    Play();
                    DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Play Pressed"));
                    break;

                case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Pause:
                    Pause();
                    DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Pause Pressed"));
                    break;

                case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Stop:
                    Stop();
                    DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Stop Pressed"));
                    break;

                default:
                    break;
            }
        }

        private void Button_Play_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            Play();
        }

        private void Button_Pause_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            Pause();
        }

        private void Button_Stop_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            Stop();
        }
    }
}

    <UserControl

                <Button Content="Stop"  Width="70" Margin="10,0,0,0" Click="Button_Stop_Click"/>
            </StackPanel>
        </Grid>

24. namespace SDKTemplate
25. {
26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }

<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<shellId: 25 completed with exit code 0>
--- RESULT ---
<a id="windowing"></a>
## Windowing: Window.Current / ApplicationView / CoreWindow → AppWindow`Window.Current`, `ApplicationView`, and `CoreWindow` are gone. Track the main window yourself and use `Microsoft.UI.Windowing.AppWindow`. See the official [windowing guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/windowing).UWP (replace this):```csharp
var win = Window.Current;
ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView().TryResizeView(new Size(800, 600));
```WinUI 3 — expose `MainWindow` from `App`:```csharp
public partial class App : Application
{
    // With <Nullable>enable</Nullable>, declare as nullable since the assignment only happens during OnLaunched.
    public static Window? MainWindow { get; private set; }    protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
    {
        var window = new MainWindow();
        MainWindow = window;
        window.Activate();
    }
}
```Consumers running after `OnLaunched` can safely null-forgive:```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow!);
```Resize via `AppWindow`:```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
var windowId = Microsoft.UI.Win32Interop.GetWindowIdFromWindow(hwnd);
var appWindow = AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId);
appWindow.Resize(new SizeInt32(800, 600));
```### Initialization order — keep MainWindow's constructor inert`App.MainWindow` (or any equivalent static window reference) is `null` for the entire duration of `new MainWindow()` — the right-hand side runs to completion BEFORE the assignment happens. Anything inside that constructor that reads the static — directly or transitively — sees `null` and throws `E_POINTER` (0x80004003) at startup. The build is clean; the analyzer is silent; only a runtime launch reveals it.APIs that commonly trip this race when called from a `Page` reached during `MainWindow` construction:- `WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow)`
- `XamlRoot` / `Content.XamlRoot` access through a static-window helper
- `PrintManagerInterop.GetForWindow(hwnd)` / `ShowPrintUIForWindowAsync(hwnd)`
- `FileOpenPicker` / `FileSavePicker` / `FolderPicker` (require an HWND)
- `ContentDialog.ShowAsync()` when the dialog's `XamlRoot` is sourced from a static
- `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(...)` chained through `App.MainWindow`
- Any custom title bar / `SetTitleBar` helper that resolves the HWND staticallySafe pattern — assign before activate, defer first navigation:```csharp
// App.xaml.cs
protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
{
    var window = new MainWindow();
    MainWindow = window;
    window.Activate();
}
```

The `MainWindow` constructor must do only `InitializeComponent` and inert setup — no navigation to a `Page` that reads `
`App.MainWindow`. Defer the first navigation until after `Activate` completes, either from a `Loaded` handler hooked in `
`InitializeComponent` or by exposing a `NavigateToInitialPage()` method and calling it from `App.OnLaunched` after the st
tatic is assigned.

```csharp
// MainWindow.xaml.cs
public MainWindow()
{
    InitializeComponent();
    this.Activated += (_, _) =>
    {
        if (!_navigated)
        {
            _navigated = true;
            RootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage));
        }
    };
}
private bool _navigated;
```

Validator catches this race with a 10s smoke launch after the build healthcheck passes — see `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1`
` Section 7.

### AppWindow API replacements

| UWP API | WinUI 3 API |
|---------|-------------|
| `ApplicationView.TryResizeView` | `AppWindow.Resize` |
| `AppWindow.TryCreateAsync` | `AppWindow.Create` |
| `AppWindow.TryShowAsync` | `AppWindow.Show` |
| `AppWindow.TryConsolidateAsync` | `AppWindow.Destroy` |
| `AppWindow.RequestMoveXxx` | `AppWindow.Move` |
| `AppWindow.RequestPresentation` | `AppWindow.SetPresenter` |
| `CoreApplicationViewTitleBar` | `AppWindowTitleBar` |
| `CoreApplicationView.TitleBar.ExtendViewIntoTitleBar` | `AppWindow.TitleBar.ExtendsContentIntoTitleBar` |

<a id="getforcurrentview"></a>
## GetForCurrentView() ReplacementsNone of the `GetForCurrentView()` patterns work in WinUI 3 desktop — there is no implicit per-view singleton.| UWP API | WinUI 3 Replacement |
|---------|---------------------|
| `ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId)` |
| `UIViewSettings.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow` properties (size, presenter) |
| `DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView()` | `XamlRoot.RasterizationScale` or Win32 `GetDpiForWindow` |
| `CoreApplication.GetCurrentView()` | Track windows manually in `App` |
| `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView()` | Wire back handling in `NavigationView` / `BackRequested` directly |
<a id="pickers"></a>
## Pickers and Win32 SurfacesUWP pickers infer the active window. WinUI 3 desktop pickers must be initialized explicitly or `ShowAsync` throws `COMException`.```csharp
var picker = new FileOpenPicker();
picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".txt");
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
WinRT.Interop.InitializeWithWindow.Initialize(picker, hwnd);
var file = await picker.PickSingleFileAsync();
```Apply the same `InitializeWithWindow.Initialize(obj, hwnd)` pattern to `FolderPicker`, `FileSavePicker`, `DataTransferManager` (Share), `PrintManager`, and other UI surfaces that target a window.

215.                     DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Pause Pressed"));
216.                     break;
217.

219.                     Stop();
220.                     DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Stop Pressed"));
221.                     break;
222. 
223.                 default:
224.                     break;
225.             }
226.         }

44. 
45. #if DEBUG
46.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
47.             {
48.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = false;
49.             }
50. #endif
51. 
52.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
53. 
54.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
55.             // just ensure that the window is active
56.             if (rootFrame == null)
57.             {
58.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
59.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
60.                 // Set the default language
61.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
62. 
63.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
64. 
65.                 if (e.PreviousExecutionState == ApplicationExecutionState.Terminated)
66.                 {
67.                     //TODO: Load state from previously suspended application
68.                 }
69. 
70.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
71.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
72.             }
73. 
74.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
75.             {
76.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
77.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
78.                 // parameter
79.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
80.             }
81. 
82.             // Ensure the current window is active
83.             Window.Current.Activate();
84. 
85.             // Some samples want access to the LaunchActivatedEventArgs.
86.             LaunchCompleted(e);
87.         }

89.         private Frame CreateRootFrame()
90.         {
91.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
92. 
93.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
94.             // just ensure that the window is active
95.             if (rootFrame == null)
96.             {
97.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
98.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
99.

101.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
102.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
103. 
104.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
105.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;

107.

No other changes are needed.\n\nAlso copy `PlaybackControl.xaml` from the shared folder to the target (but use the version I specified in step 7 above, which replaces `<MediaElement>` with `<MediaPlayerElement>`).\n\n### 10. Replace with `Windows.Media.Playback.MediaPlayerAudioCategory`.\n\nFor EACH scenario file (1-10), make these changes:\n- Remove `using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Media;` (it's not needed, AudioCategory is gone)\n- Add `using Windows.Media.Playback;`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.Movie` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.Movie`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.Media` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.Media`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.GameChat` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.GameChat`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.Speech` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.Speech`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.Communications` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.Communications`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.Alerts` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.Alerts`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.SoundEffects` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.SoundEffects`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.GameEffects` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.GameEffects`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.GameMedia` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.GameMedia`\n- Replace `AudioCategory.Other` → `MediaPlayerAudioCategory.Other`\n\nNote: `Windows.Media.Playback.MediaPlayerAudioCategory` has ALL these values.\n\n### 11. Update `AudioCategory.csproj`\n\nThe csproj needs to include the new XAML files. Add entries for:\n- `PlaybackControl.xaml` (as a Page item - but wait, it's a UserControl. In SDK-style projects, XAML files are auto-discovered, so you just need the file to exist)\n\nActually, in SDK-style WinUI 3 projects, XAML files are automatically included as `Page` items by the SDK. So you don't need to explicitly add them to the .csproj as long as they're in the project directory. HOWEVER, UserControls need to be declared as `<Page>` items in older formats, but in SDK-style projects they're auto-included.\n\nActually, in the SDK-style WinUI 3 project (using `Microsoft.NET.Sdk` with `UseWinUI=true`), ALL `.xaml` files in the project directory are automatically included. So no csproj changes are needed for the new XAML files.\n\n### Namespace resolution for `PlaybackControl` in Scenario XAML files\n\nThe Scenario XAML files reference `local:PlaybackControl` and have `xmlns:local=\"using:PlaybackManager\"`. 11. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
12. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
13. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
14. using SDKTemplate;
15. using System;
16. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Media;
17. using Windows.UI.Core;
18. using Windows.Foundation;
19. using Windows.Media;
20. 
21. namespace PlaybackManager
22. {
23.     /// <summary>
24.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
25.     /// </summary>
26.     public sealed partial class PlaybackControl
27.     {
28.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
29.         // as NotifyUser()
30.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
31. 
32.         CoreWindow cw = Window.Current.CoreWindow;
33.         bool IsPlaying = false;
34. 
35.         SystemMediaTransportControls systemMediaControls = null;
36. 
37.         public PlaybackControl()
38.         {
39.             this.InitializeComponent();
40. 
41.             // add new handlers
42.             systemMediaControls = SystemMediaTransportControls.GetForCurrentView();
43.             systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged += SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;
44.             systemMediaControls.ButtonPressed += SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed;
45.             systemMediaControls.IsPlayEnabled = true;
46.             systemMediaControls.IsPauseEnabled = true;
47.             systemMediaControls.IsStopEnabled = true;
48.             systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Closed;
49.         }
50. 
51.         ~PlaybackControl()
52.         {
53.             if (systemMediaControls != null)
54.             {
55.                 systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged -= SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;
56.                 systemMediaControls.ButtonPressed -= SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed;
57.             }
58.         }
59. 
60.         async public void Play()
61.         {
62.             await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
63.             {
64.                 OutputMedia.Play();
65.                 systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Playing;
66.             });
67.         }
68. 
69.         async public void Pause()
70.         {
71.             await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
72.             {
73.                 OutputMedia.Pause();
74.                 systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Paused;
75.             });
76.         }
77. 
78.         async public void Stop()
79.         {
80.             await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
81.             {
82.                 OutputMedia.Stop();
83.                 systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Stopped;
84.             });
85.         }
86. 
87.         public void SetAudioCategory(AudioCategory category)
88.         {
89.             OutputMedia.AudioCategory = category;
90.         }
91. 
92.         public void SetAudioDeviceType(AudioDeviceType devicetype)
93.         {
94.             OutputMedia.AudioDeviceType = devicetype;
95.         }
96. 
97.         public async void SelectFile()
98.         {
99.             Windows.Storage.Pickers.FileOpenPicker picker = new Windows.Storage.Pickers.FileOpenPicker();
100.             picker.SuggestedStartLocation = Windows.Storage.Pickers.PickerLocationId.MusicLibrary;
101.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".mp3");
102.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".mp4");
103.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".m4a");
104.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".wma");
105.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".wav");
106.             Windows.Storage.StorageFile file = await picker.PickSingleFileAsync();
107.             if (file != null)
108.             {
109.                 var stream = await file.OpenAsync(Windows.Storage.FileAccessMode.Read);
110.                 OutputMedia.AutoPlay = false;
111.                 await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
112.                 {
113.                     OutputMedia.SetSource(stream, file.ContentType);
114.                 });
115.             }
116.         }
117. 
118.         async void DisplayStatus(string text)
119.         {
120.             await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
121.             {
122.                 Status.Text += text + "\n";
123.             });
124.         }
125. 
126.         string GetTimeStampedMessage(string eventText)
127.         {
128.             return eventText + "   " + DateTime.Now.Hour.ToString() + ":" + DateTime.Now.Minute.ToString() + ":" + DateTime.Now.Second.ToString();
129.         }
130. 
131.         //If your app is playing media you feel that a user should not miss if a VOIP call comes in, you may
132.         //want to consider pausing playback when your app receives a SoundLevel(Low) notification.
133.         //A SoundLevel(Low) means your app volume has been attenuated by the system (likely for a VOIP call).
134. 
135.         string SoundLevelToString(SoundLevel level)
136.         {
137.             string LevelString;
138. 
139.             switch (level)
140.             {
141.                 case SoundLevel.Muted:
142.                     LevelString = "Muted";
143.                     break;
144.                 case SoundLevel.Low:
145.                     LevelString = "Low";
146.                     break;
147.                 case SoundLevel.Full:
148.                     LevelString = "Full";
149.                     break;
150.                 default:
151.                     LevelString = "Unknown";
152.                     break;
153.             }
154.             return LevelString;
155.         }
156. 
157.         async void AppMuted()
158.         {
159.             await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
160.             {
161.                 if (OutputMedia.CurrentState != MediaElementState.Paused)
162.                 {
163.                     IsPlaying = true;
164.                     Pause();
165.                 }
166.                 else
167.                 {
168.                     IsPlaying = false;
169.                 }
170.             });
171.             DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Muted"));
172.         }
173. 
174.         async void AppUnmuted()
175.         {
176.             await cw.Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
177.             {
178.                 if (IsPlaying)
179.                 {
180.                     Play();
181.                 }
182.             });
183.             DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Unmuted"));
184.         }
185. 
186.         void SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged(SystemMediaTransportControls sender, SystemMediaTransportControlsPropertyChangedEventArgs e)
187.         {
188.             if (e.Property == SystemMediaTransportControlsProperty.SoundLevel)
189.             {
190.                 var soundLevelState = sender.SoundLevel;
191. 
192.                 DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("App sound level is [" + SoundLevelToString(soundLevelState) + "]"));
193.                 if (soundLevelState == SoundLevel.Muted)
194.                 {
195.                     AppMuted();
196.                 }
197.                 else
198.                 {
199.                     AppUnmuted();
200.                 }
201.             }
202.         }
203. 
204.         void SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed(SystemMediaTransportControls sender, SystemMediaTransportControlsButtonPressedEventArgs e)
205.         {
206.             switch (e.Button)
207.             {
208.                 case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Play:
209.                     Play();
210.                     DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Play Pressed"));
211.                     break;
212. 
213.                 case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Pause:
214.                     Pause();
215.                     DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Pause Pressed"));
216.                     break;
217. 
218.                 case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Stop:
219.                     Stop();
220.                     DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Stop Pressed"));
221.                     break;
222. 
223.                 default:
224.                     break;
225.             }
226.         }
227. 
228.         private void Button_Play_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
229.         {
230.             Play();
231.         }
232. 
233.         private void Button_Pause_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
234.         {
235.             Pause();
236.         }
237. 
238.         private void Button_Stop_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
239.         {
240.             Stop();
241.         }
242.     }
243. }

1. using Microsoft.UI.Dispatching;
2. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
3. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
4. using System;
5. using Windows.Media;
6. using Windows.Media.Core;
7. using Windows.Media.Playback;
8. using Windows.Storage.Pickers;
9. 
10. namespace PlaybackManager
11. {
12.     public sealed partial class PlaybackControl : UserControl
13.     {
14.         private DispatcherQueue _dispatcherQueue;
15.         private bool IsPlaying = false;
16.         private SystemMediaTransportControls? systemMediaControls;
17. 
18.         public PlaybackControl()
19.         {
20.             this.InitializeComponent();
21. 
22.             _dispatcherQueue = DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread();
23. 
24.             var mediaPlayer = new MediaPlayer();
25.             mediaPlayer.AutoPlay = false;
26.             OutputMedia.SetMediaPlayer(mediaPlayer);
27. 
28.             systemMediaControls = mediaPlayer.SystemMediaTransportControls;
29.             systemMediaControls.IsPlayEnabled = true;
30.             systemMediaControls.IsPauseEnabled = true;
31.             systemMediaControls.IsStopEnabled = true;
32.             systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Closed;
33.             systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged += SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;
34.             systemMediaControls.ButtonPressed += SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed;
35.         }
36. 
37.         ~PlaybackControl()
38.         {
39.             if (systemMediaControls != null)
40.             {
41.                 systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged -= SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;
42.                 systemMediaControls.ButtonPressed -= SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed;
43.             }
44.         }
45. 
46.         public void Play()
47.         {
48.             _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>
49.             {
50.                 OutputMedia.MediaPlayer?.Play();
51.                 systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Playing;
52.             });
53.         }
54. 
55.         public void Pause()
56.         {
57.             _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>
58.             {
59.                 OutputMedia.MediaPlayer?.Pause();
60.                 systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Paused;
61.             });
62.         }
63. 
64.         public void Stop()
65.         {
66.             _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>
67.             {
68.                 var player = OutputMedia.MediaPlayer;
69.                 if (player != null)
70.                 {
71.                     player.Pause();
72.                     if (player.PlaybackSession != null)
73.                         player.PlaybackSession.Position = TimeSpan.Zero;
74.                 }
75.                 systemMediaControls.PlaybackStatus = MediaPlaybackStatus.Stopped;
76.             });
77.         }
78. 
79.         public void SetAudioCategory(MediaPlayerAudioCategory category)
80.         {
81.             if (OutputMedia.MediaPlayer != null)
82.                 OutputMedia.MediaPlayer.AudioCategory = category;
83.         }
84. 
85.         public async void SelectFile()
86.         {
87.             var picker = new FileOpenPicker();
88.             picker.SuggestedStartLocation = PickerLocationId.MusicLibrary;
89.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".mp3");
90.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".mp4");
91.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".m4a");
92.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".wma");
93.             picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".wav");
94. 
95.             var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(AudioCategory.App.MainWindow!);
96.             WinRT.Interop.InitializeWithWindow.Initialize(picker, hwnd);
97. 
98.             var file = await picker.PickSingleFileAsync();
99.             if (file != null)
100.             {
101.                 var stream = await file.OpenAsync(Windows.Storage.FileAccessMode.Read);
102.                 _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>
103.                 {
104.                     OutputMedia.MediaPlayer.Source = MediaSource.CreateFromStream(stream, file.ContentType);
105.                 });
106.             }
107.         }
108. 
109.         private void DisplayStatus(string text)
110.         {
111.             _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>
112.             {
113.                 Status.Text += text + "\n";
114.             });
115.         }
116. 
117.         private string GetTimeStampedMessage(string eventText)
118.         {
119.             return $"{eventText}   {DateTime.Now:HH:mm:ss}";
120.         }
121. 
122.         private string SoundLevelToString(SoundLevel level)
123.         {
124.             return level switch
125.             {
126.                 SoundLevel.Muted => "Muted",
127.                 SoundLevel.Low => "Low",
128.                 SoundLevel.Full => "Full",
129.                 _ => "Unknown"
130.             };
131.         }
132. 
133.         private void AppMuted()
134.         {
135.             _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>
136.             {
137.                 var state = OutputMedia.MediaPlayer?.PlaybackSession?.PlaybackState;
138.                 if (state != MediaPlaybackState.Paused)
139.                 {
140.                     IsPlaying = true;
141.                     Pause();
142.                 }
143.                 else
144.                 {
145.                     IsPlaying = false;
146.                 }
147.             });
148.             DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Muted"));
149.         }
150. 
151.         private void AppUnmuted()
152.         {
153.             _dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() =>
154.             {
155.                 if (IsPlaying)
156.                     Play();
157.             });
158.             DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Unmuted"));
159.         }
160. 
161.         private void SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged(SystemMediaTransportControls sender, SystemMediaTransportControlsPropertyChangedEventArgs e)
162.         {
163.             if (e.Property == SystemMediaTransportControlsProperty.SoundLevel)
164.             {
165.                 var soundLevelState = sender.SoundLevel;
166.                 DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage($"App sound level is [{SoundLevelToString(soundLevelState)}]"));
167.                 if (soundLevelState == SoundLevel.Muted)
168.                     AppMuted();
169.                 else
170.                     AppUnmuted();
171.             }
172.         }
173. 
174.         private void SystemMediaControls_ButtonPressed(SystemMediaTransportControls sender, SystemMediaTransportControlsButtonPressedEventArgs e)
175.         {
176.             switch (e.Button)
177.             {
178.                 case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Play:
179.                     Play();
180.                     DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Play Pressed"));
181.                     break;
182.                 case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Pause:
183.                     Pause();
184.                     DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Pause Pressed"));
185.                     break;
186.                 case SystemMediaTransportControlsButton.Stop:
187.                     Stop();
188.                     DisplayStatus(GetTimeStampedMessage("Stop Pressed"));
189.                     break;
190.             }
191.         }
192. 
193.         private void Button_Play_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => Play();
194.         private void Button_Pause_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => Pause();
195.         private void Button_Stop_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => Stop();
196.     }
